<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct an entangled state on $`\mathbb C^{10}\otimes\mathbb C^{10}`$ with zero distillable secret key for the local-instrument protocols specified here. They allow joint processing of all copies and unlimited two-way public communication, with the input as the only shared private resource and an eavesdropper holding a purification and the complete public record. The construction also disproves the unrestricted two-map PPT-composition conjecture. A separate trace-preserving PPT channel on $`M_{21}(\mathbb C)`$ has a square that is not entanglement breaking.

- **claim-entangled-zero-key-state** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-main-state)): There is an explicitly specified entangled density operator $\rho$ on $\mathbb C^{10}\otimes\mathbb C^{10}$ whose range contains no nonzero product vector and for which $K_D(\rho)=0$. More precisely, let $n\geq1$ and let $\tau_{K_AK_BE'}$ be the normalized output of any admitted completed bit-output protocol of Definition <a href="#def:protocol" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:protocol">[def:protocol]</a> on $\rho^{\otimes n}$, where $E'$ retains an initial purification and the complete public transcript. For every state $\sigma_{E'}$, $$\label{eq:main-gap} \left\lVert \tau_{K_AK_BE'}- \frac12\sum_{i=0}^1\|i,i\rangle\langle i,i\|\otimes\sigma_{E'} \right\rVert_1\geq\frac15.$$ Thus the corresponding trace distance, with its conventional factor $1/2$, is at least $1/10$.
  - Explanation: The explicit state is entangled by its product-free range, yet every admitted completed bit-output protocol remains uniformly separated from every ideal secret bit. The obstruction permits joint processing of copies and requires Eve to retain the purification and complete public transcript.
- **claim-ppt-composition-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main-pair)): There are explicitly specified PPT maps $\Phi_1,\Phi_2:M_{10}(\mathbb C)\to M_{10}(\mathbb C)$ for which $$Z=J(\Phi_2\circ\Phi_1)$$ is nonzero and its range contains no nonzero product vector in $\mathbb C^{10}\otimes\mathbb C^{10}$. In particular, the composition $\Phi_2\circ\Phi_1$ is not entanglement breaking.
  - Explanation: The explicit PPT pair has a nonzero composite Choi matrix whose range excludes every nonzero product vector. This certifies nonseparability and refutes unrestricted PPT-composition entanglement breaking without imposing trace preservation.
- **claim-ppt-channel-square-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main-channel)): There is an explicitly specified trace-preserving PPT channel $\Theta:M_{21}(\mathbb C)\to M_{21}(\mathbb C)$ whose square $\Theta\circ\Theta$ is not entanglement breaking.
  - Explanation: An explicit trace-preserving PPT channel retains an entangled Choi corner after self-composition. This conclusion concerns its square and does not rule out eventual entanglement breaking.
- **claim-ppt-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ppt-stability)): Let $F:M_{a}(\mathbb C)\to M_{b}(\mathbb C)$ be CP. Then $F^\dagger$ and $T_b\circ F\circ T_a$ are CP. Moreover, $$F\circ T_a\text{ is CP}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad T_b\circ F\text{ is CP}.$$ If $F$ is PPT, so are $T_b\circ F$, $F\circ T_a$, its adjoint, its simultaneous transpose $T_b\circ F\circ T_a$, and its transpose-adjoint $F^\sharp:=T_a\circ F^\dagger\circ T_b$. Tensor products of PPT maps are PPT. If $P$ and $Q$ are compatible CP maps, then $Q\circ F\circ P$ is PPT whenever $F$ is PPT.
  - Explanation: Complete positivity survives adjunction and simultaneous transposition. Input and output copositivity are equivalent, and the PPT property survives the listed transpose operations, tensor products, and compatible CP pre- and post-compositions.
- **claim-choi-separability-characterisation** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-choi-separable)): A linear map $F:M_{a}(\mathbb C)\to M_{b}(\mathbb C)$ is CP if and only if $J(F)\succeq0$. For a CP map, the following are equivalent:* 1. *$J(F)$ is separable;* 2. *for every $k\geq1$ and every $W\succeq0$ in $M_{k}(\mathbb C)\otimes M_{a}(\mathbb C)$, $(\mathrm{id}_k\otimes F)(W)$ is separable.* *Also, $$J(T_b\circ F)=(\mathrm{id}_a\otimes T_b)J(F),\qquad J(F\circ T_a)=(T_a\otimes\mathrm{id}_b)J(F).$$
  - Explanation: Choi positivity characterizes complete positivity. Within the CP cone, Choi separability is equivalent to separable outputs on every positive bipartite input, and input or output transposition becomes the corresponding Choi partial transpose.
- **claim-product-free-range-witness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-product-range)): Every nonzero separable positive matrix has a nonzero product vector in its range. Consequently, a nonzero positive matrix whose range contains no nonzero product vector is nonseparable.
  - Explanation: A nonzero separable positive matrix must contain a nonzero product vector in its range. Product-free range therefore witnesses entanglement, while the presence of a product vector does not certify separability.
- **claim-represented-class-tensor-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-closure)): The class $\mathscr C$ is closed under finite tensor products, after grouping all first-party systems and all second-party systems. In particular, $\omega^{\otimes n}\in\mathscr C$ for every $\omega\in\mathscr C$ and every $n\geq1$.
  - Explanation: Tensoring representing vectors and local maps preserves the representation conditions after grouping systems by party. This permits the operational obstruction to apply to joint processing of arbitrarily many copies.
- **claim-rectangular-choi-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-choi-transfer)): Let $F:M_{a}(\mathbb C)\to M_{b}(\mathbb C)$ and $G:M_{b}(\mathbb C)\to M_{c}(\mathbb C)$ be CP maps, and set $F^\sharp=T_a\circ F^\dagger\circ T_b$. Then $$\label{eq:choi-transfer} J(G\circ F)=(F^\sharp\otimes G)(\Omega_b\Omega_b^*).$$ If $F$ and $G$ are PPT and $Z=J(G\circ F)\ne0$, then $\lambda:=\operatorname{tr}Z>0$ and $$\label{eq:choi-transfer-normalized} \frac{Z}{\lambda} =(F^\sharp\otimes G) \left(\frac{\Omega_b\Omega_b^*}{\lambda}\right)\in\mathscr C \quad\text{on }\mathbb C^a\otimes\mathbb C^c.$$
  - Explanation: The composite Choi matrix is a local image of the unnormalized maximally entangled vector using the first map's transpose-adjoint. A nonzero PPT composite becomes a represented state after division by its actual Choi trace.
- **claim-positive-block-trace-norm-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-block)): Let $H$ and $J$ be square matrices, possibly of different sizes, and let $Z$ have the corresponding rectangular size. If $$\begin{pmatrix}H&Z\\ Z^*&J\end{pmatrix}\succeq0,$$ then $$\left\lVert Z\right\rVert_1\le\sqrt{\operatorname{tr}H\,\operatorname{tr}J}.$$ This estimate includes singular $H$ and $J$.
  - Explanation: Positivity of a block matrix bounds the trace norm of its off-diagonal block by the geometric mean of the diagonal traces. The argument remains valid for singular diagonal blocks.
- **claim-four-effect-coherence-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coherence)): Let $\omega\in\mathscr C$ act on $\mathbb C^p\otimes\mathbb C^q$. For arbitrary $X_0,X_1\succeq0$ in $M_{p}(\mathbb C)$ and $Y_0,Y_1\succeq0$ in $M_{q}(\mathbb C)$, put $$D_{ij}=X_i\otimes Y_j, \qquad p_{ij}=\operatorname{tr}(D_{ij}\omega), \qquad i,j\in\{0,1\}.$$ Then $$\label{eq:coherence} \left\lVert\sqrt{D_{00}}\,\omega\sqrt{D_{11}}\right\rVert_1 \le \sqrt{p_{00}p_{01}}+\sqrt{p_{10}p_{11}} +2\sqrt{p_{01}p_{10}}.$$ There is no normalization assumption on $X_0,X_1,Y_0,Y_1$.
  - Explanation: For represented states, coherence between equal-bit branches is controlled by the local-effect probabilities of disagreement. Positivity of the effects suffices; they need not form normalized measurements.
- **claim-common-transcript-local-factors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transcript)): Let $R$ be a density operator on finite-dimensional complex spaces $A\otimes B$, and let an admitted protocol produce bits $i,j\in\{0,1\}$ almost surely on $R$. Give Eve the canonical purification <a href="#eq:canonical-purification" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:canonical-purification">[eq:canonical-purification]</a>, and let her retain $E$ and the complete public transcript $T$ with value $t$ in its standard Borel space $\mathsf T$. There exist a finite positive measure $\mu$ on $\mathsf T$ and measurable positive matrices $X_0(t),X_1(t)$ on $A$ and $Y_0(t),Y_1(t)$ on $B$ such that the positive matrix-valued measure of Eve in bit branch $(i,j)$ has the density $$\label{eq:eve-blocks} \tau_{ij}(t)= \bigl(\sqrt R\,[X_i(t)\otimes Y_j(t)]\sqrt R\bigr)^\mathsf T \quad\text{with respect to }\mu.$$ The same $X_i(t)$ and $Y_j(t)$ work for all four branches outside one $\mu$-null set, and $$\sum_{i,j=0}^1\int_{\mathsf T}\operatorname{tr}\tau_{ij}(t)\,d\mu(t)=1.$$ The transpose in <a href="#eq:eve-blocks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:eve-blocks">[eq:eve-blocks]</a> is in the product basis identifying $E$ with $A\otimes B$. No invertibility of $R$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: The complete public record admits a common finite dominating measure and positive local effect densities shared by all bit branches. The resulting formula covers singular inputs and completion required only on the actual input.
- **claim-arbitrary-purification-recovery** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-purification-recovery)): Let $R$ and $\|\Psi_R\rangle$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:transcript" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transcript">13</a>, and let $\|\psi\rangle\in A\otimes B\otimes\widetilde E$ be any purification of $R$. There is a channel $\mathcal R$ from $\widetilde E$ to $E$ that recovers the canonical purification. If Alice and Bob run the same admitted protocol and Eve retains her purification and the full transcript, $\mathcal R$ acting on the purifying register and the identity acting on the transcript recover the canonical output of Lemma <a href="#lem:transcript" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transcript">13</a>. Moreover this channel maps every ideal secret-bit law with Eve in $\widetilde E T$ to an ideal secret-bit law with Eve in $ET$.
  - Explanation: A channel on Eve's purifying register recovers the canonical purification and the corresponding protocol output while preserving the transcript. It also preserves the form of every ideal secret-bit comparison law.
- **claim-root-fidelity-comparisons** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fidelity)): For arbitrary square complex matrices $A,B$ of the same size, $$\label{eq:polar-fidelity} f(A^*A,B^*B)=\left\lVert AB^*\right\rVert_1.$$ For positive matrices $H,J$ of the same size, $$\label{eq:fidelity-lower} f(H,J)\ge \frac{\operatorname{tr}H+\operatorname{tr}J-\left\lVert H-J\right\rVert_1}{2}.$$ Also $f(H^\mathsf T,J^\mathsf T)=f(H,J)$.
  - Explanation: Polar decomposition identifies root fidelity with an off-diagonal trace norm. A lower bound in terms of traces and trace-norm difference, together with transpose invariance, connects secrecy to the coherence estimate without invertibility assumptions.
- **claim-uniform-secret-bit-obstruction** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-zero-key)): Let $\omega\in\mathscr C$. For every integer $n\ge1$, let an admitted completed protocol on $\omega^{\otimes n}$ produce normalized bit registers $K_A,K_B$, and let its output $\tau_{K_AK_BE'}$ retain in $E'$ an initial purification held by Eve and the complete public transcript. The purification may be arbitrary. For every normalized state or classical-quantum law $\sigma_{E'}$, $$\label{eq:uniform-secret-bit-gap} \left\lVert\tau_{K_AK_BE'}- \frac12\sum_{i=0}^1\|i,i\rangle\langle i,i\|\otimes\sigma_{E'} \right\rVert_1\ge\frac15.$$ Thus the conventional trace distance is at least $1/10$, and $K_D(\omega)=0$ in Definition <a href="#def:distillable-key" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:distillable-key">15</a>.
  - Explanation: Every represented state has zero distillable secret key in the admitted model. A uniform gap from every ideal bit persists across tensor powers, arbitrary purifications, and complete public records.
- **claim-conditioned-abort-and-limit-obstructions** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-operational-extensions)): Consider inputs $\omega^{\otimes n}$ with $\omega\in\mathscr C$ and $n\ge1$, and admitted protocols whose normalized completed outputs retain in Eve’s register an arbitrary initial purification and the complete public transcript. The following statements extend the gap in Theorem <a href="#thm:zero-key" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:zero-key">17</a>; in item 2, the completed local output alphabet may include an abort symbol.* 1. *If $A$ is a measurable event of the complete public transcript with probability $r>0$, the normalized bit output conditioned on $A$ has distance at least $1/5$ from every ideal bit law for that accepted output. This holds for every $r>0$.* 2. *Suppose a sequence of completed protocols has a locally available abort symbol at either party. If the probability that at least one party aborts tends to zero, and the normalized non-aborting outputs approach ideal non-aborting bit laws, then replacing each local abort by the fixed bit zero would give completed bit outputs approaching ideal bits. Such a sequence is impossible for inputs in $\mathscr C$ (including any numbers of copies).* 3. *Let $\tau_k$ be completed normalized bit outputs to which the gap applies, possibly on different output spaces. Suppose their bit registers are kept fixed and their Eve registers are embedded in a common output space $F$, giving states $\widehat\tau_k$. The embeddings must preserve and permit recovery of the full initial purification and complete public records, without coarse-graining those records. In particular, an Eve-side recovery channel recovers $\tau_k$ from $\widehat\tau_k$ while leaving the bits fixed. If $\widehat\tau_k\to\tau$ in trace norm, then for every normalized law $\sigma_F$, $$\left\lVert\tau-\tfrac 12\sum_{i=0}^1 \|i,i\rangle\langle i,i\|\otimes\sigma_F\right\rVert_1\ge\frac15.$$
  - Explanation: The secret-bit gap survives normalization on any positive-probability public acceptance event. Vanishing abort probability cannot accompany convergence to ideal accepted bits, and trace-norm limits preserve the gap when Eve's full information remains recoverable.
- **claim-symmetric-exterior-tensor-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-tensor-criterion)): Let $L:M_{4}(\mathbb C)\to M_{4}(\mathbb C)$ be PPT. Suppose that $\mathcal X=\{x_1,\ldots,x_{20}\}\subset\mathbb C^4\setminus\{0\}$ represents twenty distinct projective directions and has the following properties:* 1. *$\operatorname{rank}L(P_x)<4$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$, and every nonzero homogeneous quadratic in $\mathbb C[x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3]$ vanishes on at most nine members of $\mathcal X$;* 2. *$L(P_y)$ is positive definite for some $y\in\mathbb C^4$.* *Then there are PPT maps $\Phi_1,\Phi_2:M_{10}(\mathbb C)\to M_{10}(\mathbb C)$, constructed explicitly from $L$, such that $$Z=J(\Phi_2\circ\Phi_1)\ne0,\qquad \operatorname{ran}Z\cap\{u\otimes v:u,v\in\mathbb C^{10}\}=\{0\}.$$ In particular, $\Phi_2\circ\Phi_1$ is not entanglement breaking.
  - Explanation: Singular outputs generate product vectors in the composite Choi kernel through exterior-square duality. Independence of quadratic evaluations prevents the zero sets of the factors of any proposed range product from covering all singular directions; a positive-definite output ensures nonzero Choi mass.
- **claim-integer-pencil-ppt-and-rank** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pencil-ppt)): The map $L:M_{4}(\mathbb C)\to M_{4}(\mathbb C)$ in <a href="#eq:L" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:L">[eq:L]</a> is PPT. For every $x\in\mathbb C^4$, $$L(P_x)=M(x)^{\mathsf T}\overline{M(x)}=M(x)^*M(x),\qquad \operatorname{rank}L(P_x)=\operatorname{rank}M(x). \label{eq:gram-identity}$$ Moreover, $L(P_{e_0})=36I_4$.
  - Explanation: The displayed integer pencil defines a PPT map by a Kraus representation and block symmetry. Its rank-one outputs are Gram matrices with the same rank as the pencil, and the coordinate input supplies a positive-definite output.
- **claim-twenty-directions-quadratic-independence** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-twenty-directions)): The projective directions $[x]\in\mathbb P^3(\mathbb C)$ for which $\operatorname{rank}M(x)<4$ consist of exactly twenty distinct points. Each has a unique representative $x=(1,t_1,t_2,t_3)$. Every nonzero homogeneous quadratic in $\mathbb C[x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3]$ vanishes on at most nine of these representatives.
  - Explanation: All projective rank-loss directions lie in the specified affine chart and are distinct. Every selection of ten directions gives independent quadratic evaluations, so a nonzero homogeneous quadratic can vanish at no more than nine of them.
- **claim-six-bilinear-common-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-six-bilinear)): Any six complex bilinear forms on $\mathbb C^4\times\mathbb C^4$ have a common zero $(x,y)$ with $x\ne0$ and $y\ne0$.
  - Explanation: Every system of the specified number of complex bilinear forms has a common zero with both factors nonzero. The height argument establishes existence rather than an explicit procedure for finding the zero.
- **claim-monic-local-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-monic-local-reduction)): Let $p$ be a prime, and let the coefficient field and its valuation ring be one of the pairs $$(\mathbb L,R_p)=(\mathbb Q,\mathbb Z_{(p)}) \quad\text{or}\quad (\mathbb L,R_p)=(\mathbb Q_p,\mathbb Z_p).$$ If a monic polynomial $P\in R_p[z]$ factors as $P=gh$ with monic $g,h\in\mathbb L[z]$, then $g,h\in R_p[z]$, and their reductions modulo $p$ retain their degrees, including degree zero. Consequently, if $P$ has positive degree and irreducible reduction in $\mathbb F_p[z]$, then $P$ is irreducible over $\mathbb L$.
  - Explanation: Monic factors over either fraction field retain integral coefficients and degrees after reduction. An irreducible positive-degree reduction therefore certifies irreducibility over the fraction field even when coefficients are only locally integral.
- **claim-local-frobenius-cycle-types** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-frobenius)): Let $F\in\mathbb Q[z]$ be monic, separable, and of positive degree, with coefficients in $\mathbb Z_{(p)}$. If its reduction in $\mathbb F_p[z]$ is squarefree with irreducible-factor degrees $s_1,\ldots,s_h$, then the Galois group of its rational splitting field contains an element with cycle lengths $s_1,\ldots,s_h$ on the roots.
  - Explanation: Squarefree factor degrees at a locally integral prime supply a permutation of the rational splitting-field roots with those cycle lengths. The proof lifts residue roots and restricts the resulting local automorphism to the rational splitting field.
- **claim-trace-preserving-flag-completion** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-channel-completion)): Let $\Psi_1,\Psi_2:M_{m}(\mathbb C)\to M_{m}(\mathbb C)$ be PPT maps. There are explicitly defined constants $c_1,c_2>0$, a trace-preserving PPT channel $\Theta:M_{2m+1}(\mathbb C)\to M_{2m+1}(\mathbb C)$, and a real coordinate isometry $W_0:\mathbb C^m\to\mathbb C^{2m+1}$ such that $$\label{eq:choi-corner} (W_0^*\otimes W_0^*)J(\Theta\circ\Theta)(W_0\otimes W_0) =c_1c_2J(\Psi_2\circ\Psi_1).$$ If $J(\Psi_2\circ\Psi_1)$ is nonseparable, then $\Theta\circ\Theta$ is not entanglement breaking.
  - Explanation: Rescaling a square PPT pair, alternating between coordinate blocks, and directing missing trace to an absorbing flag gives a trace-preserving PPT channel. A local Choi corner of its square reproduces the original composite up to positive scaling.
- **claim-rectangular-projection-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-projection-criterion)): Let $Q,K,D$ be positive integers, let $\mathcal Q$ be a set of $Q$ elements, and let $u_q\in\mathbb R^K$, $q\in\mathcal Q$, be unit vectors. Put $c_{qr}=u_q^{\mathsf T}u_r$. For each $q$, let $\{P_i:i\in\mathcal I_q\}$ be a finite projective measurement on $\mathbb C^D$: its members are self-adjoint projections, they are mutually orthogonal, and their sum is $I_D$. Zero projections are allowed. Take the label sets $\mathcal I_q$ disjoint, put $\mathcal I=\bigsqcup_{q\in\mathcal Q}\mathcal I_q$, and set $n=\|\mathcal I\|$. Suppose that* 1. *$P_iP_j=P_jP_i$ whenever $i\in\mathcal I_q$, $j\in\mathcal I_r$, and $c_{qr}\ne0$;* 2. *there is a real number $\kappa<Q/K$ such that every $J\subseteq\mathcal I$ satisfying $\operatorname{tr}(P_iP_j)>0$ for all distinct $i,j\in J$ has $\|J\|\leq\kappa$.* *After ordering $\mathcal I$, define the complex-linear map $\Psi:M_{n}(\mathbb C)\to M_{D}(\mathbb C)$ on matrix units by $$\label{eq:projection-map} H_{ij}=c_{qr}P_iP_j,\qquad \Psi(E_{ij})=H_{ij} \quad(i\in\mathcal I_q,\ j\in\mathcal I_r).$$ Then $\Psi$ and its Hilbert–Schmidt adjoint $\Psi^\dagger:M_{D}(\mathbb C)\to M_{n}(\mathbb C)$ are PPT. Moreover, $$\label{eq:projection-composite} C:=J(\Psi^\dagger\circ\Psi) \quad\text{is nonseparable over }\mathbb C^n\otimes\mathbb C^n, \qquad \operatorname{tr}C=Q^2D.$$
  - Explanation: Real unit-vector correlations and compatible projection products form a positive Choi block Gram matrix with positive partial transpose. A strict overlap-support bound contradicts the lower Gram bound required by the construction, proving complex nonseparability of the adjoint composition.
- **claim-identical-real-local-channel** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-real-channel)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:projection-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:projection-criterion">26</a>, suppose that every $P_i$ is real symmetric in the fixed basis of $\mathbb C^D$, and put $\Lambda=\Psi^\dagger:M_{D}(\mathbb C)\to M_{n}(\mathbb C)$. Then, on all complex inputs, $$\label{eq:projection-real-invariances} \Lambda\circ T_D=\Lambda=T_n\circ\Lambda, \qquad \operatorname{tr}\Lambda(A)=Q\,\operatorname{tr}A.$$ In particular, $\Lambda/Q$ is a trace-preserving PPT channel. For $\Phi_D=\Omega_D/\sqrt D$, the normalized maximally entangled vector on $\mathbb C^D\otimes\mathbb C^D$, one has $$\label{eq:projection-real-state} \frac{C}{Q^2D} = \left(\frac{\Lambda}{Q}\otimes\frac{\Lambda}{Q}\right) (\Phi_D\Phi_D^*).$$ This is an entangled density operator on $\mathbb C^n\otimes\mathbb C^n$ in $\mathscr C$.
  - Explanation: Real symmetric projections make the adjoint invariant under input and output transpose. After trace normalization, the same rectangular PPT channel applied in parallel to both halves of a maximally entangled state produces an entangled represented state.
- **claim-square-embedding** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-square-embedding)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:projection-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:projection-criterion">26</a>, suppose in addition that $n\geq D$, and let $U:\mathbb C^D\to\mathbb C^n$ be the real coordinate isometry onto the first $D$ coordinates. Define $$\label{eq:projection-square-pair} \Xi_1=\operatorname{Ad}_U\circ\Psi,\qquad \Xi_2=\Psi^\dagger\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{U^*} =\Xi_1^\dagger .$$ Then $\Xi_1,\Xi_2:M_{n}(\mathbb C)\to M_{n}(\mathbb C)$ are PPT and $$\Xi_2\circ\Xi_1=\Psi^\dagger\circ\Psi.$$ Their composition is not entanglement breaking.
  - Explanation: A coordinate embedding converts the rectangular projection map and its adjoint into square PPT maps without changing their composite. The dimension comparison permits the isometry and is not required by the rectangular criterion itself.
- **claim-restricted-intersection-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-restricted-intersections)): If $\mathcal F\subseteq\mathcal U$ contains no two distinct sets whose associated vectors are orthogonal, then $\|\mathcal F\|\leq a_0=\delta b$.
  - Explanation: A family lacking an orthogonal pair is bounded by the dimension of a space of low-degree multilinear polynomials over the prime field. Evaluation at indicator vectors proves linear independence of the associated polynomials.
- **claim-fiber-orthogonality-amplification** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fiber-amplification)): For $t=1,2,3$, associate to $(A_1,\ldots,A_t)\in\mathcal U^t$ the unit vector $s_{A_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes s_{A_t}$. If $\mathcal R\subseteq\mathcal U^t$ contains no collection of $2^t$ distinct tuples whose associated vectors are pairwise orthogonal, then $$\|\mathcal R\|\leq t\delta b^t.$$
  - Explanation: Induction over tensor factors bounds a subfamily without a sufficiently large pairwise orthogonal collection. Fibers rich in orthogonal collections cannot themselves have orthogonal first-factor labels.
- **claim-parity-positive-overlap-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-parity-obstruction)): There is no choice of one projection from each of the six measurements in <a href="#eq:combinatorial-context-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:combinatorial-context-projections">[eq:combinatorial-context-projections]</a> such that the trace overlap of every two chosen projections from distinct contexts is strictly positive.
  - Explanation: Positive overlap would force row and column eigensigns to agree at every shared observable. The opposing total parity constraints make such a selection impossible over the complex Hilbert space.
- **claim-global-measurement-hypotheses** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-combinatorial-measurements)): The vectors <a href="#eq:combinatorial-questions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:combinatorial-questions">[eq:combinatorial-questions]</a> and measurements <a href="#eq:combinatorial-global-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:combinatorial-global-projections">[eq:combinatorial-global-projections]</a> satisfy the two hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:projection-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:projection-criterion">26</a> with $n=m=QD$ and $\kappa=3a_0b^2<Q/K$.
  - Explanation: The tensor-assembled measurements commute whenever question correlations are nonzero. Any pairwise positive-overlap label family excludes an orthogonal eight-set and therefore obeys the strict support bound needed by the projection criterion.
- **claim-finite-combinatorial-state-pair-channel** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-old-construction)): Set $$\label{eq:combinatorial-exact-data} \begin{gathered} p=257,\qquad k=1028,\qquad b=\binom{1027}{513},\qquad a_0=\sum_{j=0}^{256}\binom{1028}{j},\\ Q=b^3,\qquad K=1086373952,\qquad \kappa=3a_0b^2<\frac QK,\\ L=\binom Q8,\qquad D=4^L,\qquad m=QD. \end{gathered}$$ Use the vectors in <a href="#eq:combinatorial-questions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:combinatorial-questions">[eq:combinatorial-questions]</a>, the $D$ labels per question and projections in <a href="#eq:combinatorial-global-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:combinatorial-global-projections">[eq:combinatorial-global-projections]</a>, and the map $\Psi:M_{m}(\mathbb C)\to M_{D}(\mathbb C)$ in <a href="#eq:projection-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projection-map">[eq:projection-map]</a>. Put $\Lambda=\Psi^\dagger$ and $\Phi_D=\Omega_D/\sqrt D$.* 1. *The map $\Lambda/Q:M_{D}(\mathbb C)\to M_{m}(\mathbb C)$ is a trace-preserving PPT channel, and $$\label{eq:combinatorial-state} \rho_{\mathrm{comb}} =\left(\frac{\Lambda}{Q}\otimes\frac{\Lambda}{Q}\right) (\Phi_D\Phi_D^*) =\frac{J(\Psi^\dagger\circ\Psi)}{Q^2D}$$ is an entangled density operator on $\mathbb C^m\otimes\mathbb C^m$ belonging to $\mathscr C$. It has $K_D(\rho_{\mathrm{comb}})=0$ and satisfies the uniform unhalved $1/5$ gap and all operational consequences of Theorem <a href="#thm:zero-key" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:zero-key">17</a> and Proposition <a href="#prop:operational-extensions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:operational-extensions">18</a>, with their stated protocol, purification, and completion hypotheses.* 2. *For the real coordinate isometry $U:\mathbb C^D\to\mathbb C^m$ onto the first $D$ coordinates, the maps $$\Phi_1^{\mathrm{comb}}=\operatorname{Ad}_U\circ\Psi,\qquad \Phi_2^{\mathrm{comb}}=\Lambda\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{U^*} =(\Phi_1^{\mathrm{comb}})^\dagger$$ are PPT maps on $M_{m}(\mathbb C)$, and $$\label{eq:combinatorial-pair} J(\Phi_2^{\mathrm{comb}}\circ\Phi_1^{\mathrm{comb}}) =Q^2D\,\rho_{\mathrm{comb}}$$ is nonseparable over $\mathbb C^m\otimes\mathbb C^m$. In particular their composition is not entanglement breaking.* 3. *Let $\Theta_{\mathrm{comb}}$ be the channel obtained by applying Proposition <a href="#prop:channel-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:channel-completion">25</a> to this square pair. It is a trace-preserving PPT channel on $M_{2m+1}(\mathbb C)$. With the positive constants $c_1,c_2$ and the first coordinate inclusion $W_0$ from that proposition, the Choi matrix of its square has the corner $$\label{eq:combinatorial-channel-corner} (W_0^*\otimes W_0^*)J(\Theta_{\mathrm{comb}}^2) (W_0\otimes W_0) =c_1c_2Q^2D\,\rho_{\mathrm{comb}}.$$ Consequently $\Theta_{\mathrm{comb}}^2 =\Theta_{\mathrm{comb}}\circ\Theta_{\mathrm{comb}}$ is not entanglement breaking.
  - Explanation: The finite combinatorial formulas produce an entangled represented state from identical parallel rectangular channel actions, a square PPT pair with nonseparable composite Choi matrix, and a trace-preserving PPT channel with non-entanglement-breaking square. The state inherits the operational zero-key obstruction.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ppt-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F:M_{a}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{b}(\\mathbb C)$ be CP. Then $F^\\dagger$ and $T_b\\circ F\\circ T_a$ are CP. Moreover, $$F\\circ T_a\\text{ is CP}\\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n T_b\\circ F\\text{ is CP}.$$ If $F$ is PPT, so are $T_b\\circ F$, $F\\circ T_a$, its adjoint, its simultaneous transpose $T_b\\circ F\\circ T_a$, and its transpose-adjoint $F^\\sharp:=T_a\\circ F^\\dagger\\circ T_b$. Tensor products of PPT maps are PPT. If $P$ and $Q$ are compatible CP maps, then $Q\\circ F\\circ P$ is PPT whenever $F$ is PPT.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-choi-separable",
        "exact_quote": "A linear map $F:M_{a}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{b}(\\mathbb C)$ is CP if and only if $J(F)\\succeq0$. For a CP map, the following are equivalent:*\n\n1.  *$J(F)$ is separable;*\n\n2.  *for every $k\\geq1$ and every $W\\succeq0$ in $M_{k}(\\mathbb C)\\otimes M_{a}(\\mathbb C)$, $(\\mathrm{id}_k\\otimes F)(W)$ is separable.*\n\n*Also, $$J(T_b\\circ F)=(\\mathrm{id}_a\\otimes T_b)J(F),\\qquad\n J(F\\circ T_a)=(T_a\\otimes\\mathrm{id}_b)J(F).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-choi-separable",
          "exact_quote": "A linear map $F:M_{a}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{b}(\\mathbb C)$ is CP if and only if $J(F)\\succeq0$.           "source_id": "PM-272.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-choi-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F:M_{a}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{b}(\\mathbb C)$ and $G:M_{b}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{c}(\\mathbb C)$ be CP maps, and set $F^\\sharp=T_a\\circ F^\\dagger\\circ T_b$. Then $$\\label{eq:choi-transfer}\n J(G\\circ F)=(F^\\sharp\\otimes G)(\\Omega_b\\Omega_b^*).$$ If $F$ and $G$ are PPT and $Z=J(G\\circ F)\\ne0$, then $\\lambda:=\\operatorname{tr}Z>0$ and $$\\label{eq:choi-transfer-normalized}\n \\frac{Z}{\\lambda}\n =(F^\\sharp\\otimes G)\n \\left(\\frac{\\Omega_b\\Omega_b^*}{\\lambda}\\right)\\in\\mathscr C\n \\quad\\text{on }\\mathbb C^a\\otimes\\mathbb C^c.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-coherence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega\\in\\mathscr C$ act on $\\mathbb C^p\\otimes\\mathbb C^q$. For arbitrary $X_0,X_1\\succeq0$ in $M_{p}(\\mathbb C)$ and $Y_0,Y_1\\succeq0$ in $M_{q}(\\mathbb C)$, put $$D_{ij}=X_i\\otimes Y_j,\n \\qquad p_{ij}=\\operatorname{tr}(D_{ij}\\omega),\n \\qquad i,j\\in\\{0,1\\}.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:coherence}\n \\left\\lVert\\sqrt{D_{00}}\\,\\omega\\sqrt{D_{11}}\\right\\rVert_1\n \\le \\sqrt{p_{00}p_{01}}+\\sqrt{p_{10}p_{11}}\n       +2\\sqrt{p_{01}p_{10}}.$$ There is no normalization assumption on $X_0,X_1,Y_0,Y_1$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transcript",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a density operator on finite-dimensional complex spaces $A\\otimes B$, and let an admitted protocol produce bits $i,j\\in\\{0,1\\}$ almost surely on $R$. Give Eve the canonical purification <a href=\"#eq:canonical-purification\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:canonical-purification\">[eq:canonical-purification]</a>, and let her retain $E$ and the complete public transcript $T$ with value $t$ in its standard Borel space $\\mathsf T$. There exist a finite positive measure $\\mu$ on $\\mathsf T$ and measurable positive matrices $X_0(t),X_1(t)$ on $A$ and $Y_0(t),Y_1(t)$ on $B$ such that the positive matrix-valued measure of Eve in bit branch $(i,j)$ has the density $$\\label{eq:eve-blocks}\n \\tau_{ij}(t)=\n \\bigl(\\sqrt R\\,[X_i(t)\\otimes Y_j(t)]\\sqrt R\\bigr)^\\mathsf T\n \\quad\\text{with respect to }\\mu.$$ The same $X_i(t)$ and $Y_j(t)$ work for all four branches outside one $\\mu$-null set, and $$\\sum_{i,j=0}^1\\int_{\\mathsf T}\\operatorname{tr}\\tau_{ij}(t)\\,d\\mu(t)=1.$$ The transpose in <a href=\"#eq:eve-blocks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:eve-blocks\">[eq:eve-blocks]</a> is in the product basis identifying $E$ with $A\\otimes B$. No invertibility of $R$ is assumed.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transcript",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a density operator on finite-dimensional complex spaces $A\\otimes B$, and let an admitted protocol produce bits $i,j\\in\\{0,1\\}$ almost surely on $R$. There exist a finite positive measure $\\mu$ on $\\mathsf T$ and measurable positive matrices $X_0(t),X_1(t)$ on $A$ and $Y_0(t),Y_1(t)$ on $B$ such that the positive matrix-valued measure of Eve in bit branch $(i,j)$ has the density $$\\label{eq:eve-blocks}\n \\tau_{ij}(t)=\n \\bigl(\\sqrt R\\,[X_i(t)\\otimes Y_j(t)]\\sqrt R\\bigr)^\\mathsf T\n \\quad\\text{with respect to }\\mu.$$ The same $X_i(t)$ and $Y_j(t)$ work for all four branches outside one $\\mu$-null set, and $$\\sum_{i,j=0}^1\\int_{\\mathsf T}\\operatorname{tr}\\tau_{ij}(t)\\,d\\mu(t)=1.$$ The transpose in <a href=\"#eq:eve-blocks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:eve-blocks\">[eq:eve-blocks]</a> is in the product basis identifying $E$ with $A\\otimes B$. No invertibility of $R$ is assumed.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $R$ be a density operator on finite-dimensional complex spaces $A\\otimes B$, and let an admitted protocol produce bits $i,j\\in\\{0,1\\}$ almost surely on $R$. Give Eve the canonical purification <a href=\"#eq:canonical-purification\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:canonical-purification\">[eq:canonical-purification]</a>, and let her retain $E$ and the complete public transcript $T$ with value $t$ in its standard Borel space $\\mathsf T$. There exist a finite positive measure $\\mu$ on $\\mathsf T$ and measurable positive matrices $X_0(t),X_1(t)$ on $A$ and $Y_0(t),Y_1(t)$ on $B$ such that the positive matrix-valued measure of Eve in bit branch $(i,j)$ has the density $$\\label{eq:eve-blocks}\n \\tau_{ij}(t)=\n \\bigl(\\sqrt R\\,[X_i(t)\\otimes Y_j(t)]\\sqrt R\\bigr)^\\mathsf T\n \\quad\\text{with respect to }\\mu.$$ The same $X_i(t)$ and $Y_j(t)$ work for all four branches outside one $\\mu$-null set, and $$\\sum_{i,j=0}^1\\int_{\\mathsf T}\\operatorname{tr}\\tau_{ij}(t)\\,d\\mu(t)=1.$$ The transpose in <a href=\"#eq:eve-blocks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:eve-blocks\">[eq:eve-blocks]</a> is in the product basis identifying $E$ with $A\\otimes B$. No invertibility of $R$ is assumed.",
      "explanation": "The complete public record admits a common finite dominating measure and positive local effect densities shared by all bit branches. The resulting formula covers singular inputs and completion required only on the actual input.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-purification-recovery",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ and $|\\Psi_R\\rangle$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:transcript\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:transcript\">13</a>, and let $|\\psi\\rangle\\in A\\otimes B\\otimes\\widetilde E$ be any purification of $R$. There is a channel $\\mathcal R$ from $\\widetilde E$ to $E$ that recovers the canonical purification. If Alice and Bob run the same admitted protocol and Eve retains her purification and the full transcript, $\\mathcal R$ acting on the purifying register and the identity acting on the transcript recover the canonical output of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:transcript\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:transcript\">13</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fidelity",
        "exact_quote": "For arbitrary square complex matrices $A,B$ of the same size, $$\\label{eq:polar-fidelity}\n f(A^*A,B^*B)=\\left\\lVert AB^*\\right\\rVert_1.$$ For positive matrices $H,J$ of the same size, $$\\label{eq:fidelity-lower}\n f(H,J)\\ge\n \\frac{\\operatorname{tr}H+\\operatorname{tr}J-\\left\\lVert H-J\\right\\rVert_1}{2}.$$ Also $f(H^\\mathsf T,J^\\mathsf T)=f(H,J)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fidelity",
          "exact_quote": "For arbitrary square complex matrices $A,B$ of the same size, $$\\label{eq:polar-fidelity}\n f(A^*A,B^*B)=\\left\\lVert AB^*\\right\\rVert_1.$$ For positive matrices $H,J$ of the same size, $$\\label{eq:fidelity-lower}\n f(H,J)\\ge\n \\frac{\\operatorname{tr}H+\\operatorname{tr}J-\\left\\lVert H-J\\right\\rVert_1}{2}.$$ Also $f(H^\\mathsf T,J^\\mathsf T)=f(H,J)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-zero-key",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega\\in\\mathscr C$. For every integer $n\\ge1$, let an admitted completed protocol on $\\omega^{\\otimes n}$ produce normalized bit registers $K_A,K_B$, and let its output $\\tau_{K_AK_BE'}$ retain in $E'$ an initial purification held by Eve and the complete public transcript. The purification may be arbitrary. For every normalized state or classical-quantum law $\\sigma_{E'}$, $$\\label{eq:uniform-secret-bit-gap}\n \\left\\lVert\\tau_{K_AK_BE'}-\n  \\frac12\\sum_{i=0}^1|i,i\\rangle\\langle i,i|\\otimes\\sigma_{E'}\n \\right\\rVert_1\\ge\\frac15.$$ Thus the conventional trace distance is at least $1/10$, and $K_D(\\omega)=0$ in Definition <a href=\"#def:distillable-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:distillable-key\">15</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-zero-key",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega\\in\\mathscr C$. The purification may be arbitrary. For every normalized state or classical-quantum law $\\sigma_{E'}$, $$\\label{eq:uniform-secret-bit-gap}\n \\left\\lVert\\tau_{K_AK_BE'}-\n  \\frac12\\sum_{i=0}^1|i,i\\rangle\\langle i,i|\\otimes\\sigma_{E'}\n \\right\\rVert_1\\ge\\frac15.$$ Thus the conventional trace distance is at least $1/10$, and $K_D(\\omega)=0$ in Definition <a href=\"#def:distillable-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:distillable-key\">15</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-operational-extensions",
        "exact_quote": "Consider inputs $\\omega^{\\otimes n}$ with $\\omega\\in\\mathscr C$ and $n\\ge1$, and admitted protocols whose normalized completed outputs retain in Eve’s register an arbitrary initial purification and the complete public transcript. The following statements extend the gap in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:zero-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:zero-key\">17</a>; in item 2, the completed local output alphabet may include an abort symbol.*\n\n1.  *If $A$ is a measurable event of the complete public transcript with probability $r>0$, the normalized bit output conditioned on $A$ has distance at least $1/5$ from every ideal bit law for that accepted output. This holds for every $r>0$.*\n\n2.  *Suppose a sequence of completed protocols has a locally available abort symbol at either party. If the probability that at least one party aborts tends to zero, and the normalized non-aborting outputs approach ideal non-aborting bit laws, then replacing each local abort by the fixed bit zero would give completed bit outputs approaching ideal bits. Such a sequence is impossible for inputs in $\\mathscr C$ (including any numbers of copies).*\n\n3.  *Let $\\tau_k$ be completed normalized bit outputs to which the gap applies, possibly on different output spaces. Suppose their bit registers are kept fixed and their Eve registers are embedded in a common output space $F$, giving states $\\widehat\\tau_k$. The embeddings must preserve and permit recovery of the full initial purification and complete public records, without coarse-graining those records. In particular, an Eve-side recovery channel recovers $\\tau_k$ from $\\widehat\\tau_k$ while leaving the bits fixed. If $\\widehat\\tau_k\\to\\tau$ in trace norm, then for every normalized law $\\sigma_F$, $$\\left\\lVert\\tau-\\tfrac 12\\sum_{i=0}^1\n           |i,i\\rangle\\langle i,i|\\otimes\\sigma_F\\right\\rVert_1\\ge\\frac15.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-operational-extensions",
          "exact_quote": "Consider inputs $\\omega^{\\otimes n}$ with $\\omega\\in\\mathscr C$ and $n\\ge1$, and admitted protocols whose normalized completed outputs retain in Eve’s register an arbitrary initial purification and the complete public transcript. This holds for every $r>0$.*\n\n2.  *Suppose a sequence of completed protocols has a locally available abort symbol at either party. If the probability that at least one party aborts tends to zero, and the normalized non-aborting outputs approach ideal non-aborting bit laws, then replacing each local abort by the fixed bit zero would give completed bit outputs approaching ideal bits. Such a sequence is impossible for inputs in $\\mathscr C$ (including any numbers of copies).*\n\n3.  *Let $\\tau_k$ be completed normalized bit outputs to which the gap applies, possibly on different output spaces. Suppose their bit registers are kept fixed and their Eve registers are embedded in a common output space $F$, giving states $\\widehat\\tau_k$. The embeddings must preserve and permit recovery of the full initial purification and complete public records, without coarse-graining those records. In particular, an Eve-side recovery channel recovers $\\tau_k$ from $\\widehat\\tau_k$ while leaving the bits fixed. If $\\widehat\\tau_k\\to\\tau$ in trace norm, then for every normalized law $\\sigma_F$, $$\\left\\lVert\\tau-\\tfrac 12\\sum_{i=0}^1\n           |i,i\\rangle\\langle i,i|\\otimes\\sigma_F\\right\\rVert_1\\ge\\frac15.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        }

Such a sequence is impossible for inputs in $\\mathscr C$ (including any numbers of copies).*\n\n3.  *Let $\\tau_k$ be completed normalized bit outputs to which the gap applies, possibly on different output spaces.       "explanation": "The secret-bit gap survives normalization on any positive-probability public acceptance event. Vanishing abort probability cannot accompany convergence to ideal accepted bits, and trace-norm limits preserve the gap when Eve's full information remains recoverable.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tensor-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L:M_{4}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{4}(\\mathbb C)$ be PPT. Suppose that $\\mathcal X=\\{x_1,\\ldots,x_{20}\\}\\subset\\mathbb C^4\\setminus\\{0\\}$ represents twenty distinct projective directions and has the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\operatorname{rank}L(P_x)<4$ for every $x\\in\\mathcal X$, and every nonzero homogeneous quadratic in $\\mathbb C[x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3]$ vanishes on at most nine members of $\\mathcal X$;*\n\n2.  *$L(P_y)$ is positive definite for some $y\\in\\mathbb C^4$.*\n\n*Then there are PPT maps $\\Phi_1,\\Phi_2:M_{10}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{10}(\\mathbb C)$, constructed explicitly from $L$, such that $$Z=J(\\Phi_2\\circ\\Phi_1)\\ne0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{ran}Z\\cap\\{u\\otimes v:u,v\\in\\mathbb C^{10}\\}=\\{0\\}.$$ In particular, $\\Phi_2\\circ\\Phi_1$ is not entanglement breaking.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tensor-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L:M_{4}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{4}(\\mathbb C)$ be PPT.       "exact_statement": "Let $L:M_{4}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{4}(\\mathbb C)$ be PPT. Suppose that $\\mathcal X=\\{x_1,\\ldots,x_{20}\\}\\subset\\mathbb C^4\\setminus\\{0\\}$ represents twenty distinct projective directions and has the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\operatorname{rank}L(P_x)<4$ for every $x\\in\\mathcal X$, and every nonzero homogeneous quadratic in $\\mathbb C[x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3]$ vanishes on at most nine members of $\\mathcal X$;*\n\n2.  *$L(P_y)$ is positive definite for some $y\\in\\mathbb C^4$.*\n\n*Then there are PPT maps $\\Phi_1,\\Phi_2:M_{10}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{10}(\\mathbb C)$, constructed explicitly from $L$, such that $$Z=J(\\Phi_2\\circ\\Phi_1)\\ne0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{ran}Z\\cap\\{u\\otimes v:u,v\\in\\mathbb C^{10}\\}=\\{0\\}.$$ In particular, $\\Phi_2\\circ\\Phi_1$ is not entanglement breaking.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-monic-local-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be a prime, and let the coefficient field and its valuation ring be one of the pairs $$(\\mathbb L,R_p)=(\\mathbb Q,\\mathbb Z_{(p)})\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad\n (\\mathbb L,R_p)=(\\mathbb Q_p,\\mathbb Z_p).$$ If a monic polynomial $P\\in R_p[z]$ factors as $P=gh$ with monic $g,h\\in\\mathbb L[z]$, then $g,h\\in R_p[z]$, and their reductions modulo $p$ retain their degrees, including degree zero. Consequently, if $P$ has positive degree and irreducible reduction in $\\mathbb F_p[z]$, then $P$ is irreducible over $\\mathbb L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-channel-completion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Psi_1,\\Psi_2:M_{m}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$ be PPT maps. There are explicitly defined constants $c_1,c_2>0$, a trace-preserving PPT channel $\\Theta:M_{2m+1}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{2m+1}(\\mathbb C)$, and a real coordinate isometry $W_0:\\mathbb C^m\\to\\mathbb C^{2m+1}$ such that $$\\label{eq:choi-corner}\n (W_0^*\\otimes W_0^*)J(\\Theta\\circ\\Theta)(W_0\\otimes W_0)\n       =c_1c_2J(\\Psi_2\\circ\\Psi_1).$$ If $J(\\Psi_2\\circ\\Psi_1)$ is nonseparable, then $\\Theta\\circ\\Theta$ is not entanglement breaking.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },

      "explanation": "Rescaling a square PPT pair, alternating between coordinate blocks, and directing missing trace to an absorbing flag gives a trace-preserving PPT channel. A local Choi corner of its square reproduces the original composite up to positive scaling.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-projection-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q,K,D$ be positive integers, let $\\mathcal Q$ be a set of $Q$ elements, and let $u_q\\in\\mathbb R^K$, $q\\in\\mathcal Q$, be unit vectors. Put $c_{qr}=u_q^{\\mathsf T}u_r$. For each $q$, let $\\{P_i:i\\in\\mathcal I_q\\}$ be a finite projective measurement on $\\mathbb C^D$: its members are self-adjoint projections, they are mutually orthogonal, and their sum is $I_D$. Zero projections are allowed. Take the label sets $\\mathcal I_q$ disjoint, put $\\mathcal I=\\bigsqcup_{q\\in\\mathcal Q}\\mathcal I_q$, and set $n=|\\mathcal I|$. Suppose that*\n\n1.  *$P_iP_j=P_jP_i$ whenever $i\\in\\mathcal I_q$, $j\\in\\mathcal I_r$, and $c_{qr}\\ne0$;*\n\n2.  *there is a real number $\\kappa<Q/K$ such that every $J\\subseteq\\mathcal I$ satisfying $\\operatorname{tr}(P_iP_j)>0$ for all distinct $i,j\\in J$ has $|J|\\leq\\kappa$.*\n\n\n*After ordering $\\mathcal I$, define the complex-linear map $\\Psi:M_{n}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{D}(\\mathbb C)$ on matrix units by $$\\label{eq:projection-map}\n H_{ij}=c_{qr}P_iP_j,\\qquad\n \\Psi(E_{ij})=H_{ij}\n \\quad(i\\in\\mathcal I_q,\\ j\\in\\mathcal I_r).$$ Then $\\Psi$ and its Hilbert–Schmidt adjoint $\\Psi^\\dagger:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{n}(\\mathbb C)$ are PPT. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:projection-composite}\n C:=J(\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\Psi)\n \\quad\\text{is nonseparable over }\\mathbb C^n\\otimes\\mathbb C^n,\n \\qquad \\operatorname{tr}C=Q^2D.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-projection-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Q,K,D$ be positive integers, let $\\mathcal Q$ be a set of $Q$ elements, and let $u_q\\in\\mathbb R^K$, $q\\in\\mathcal Q$, be unit vectors. For each $q$, let $\\{P_i:i\\in\\mathcal I_q\\}$ be a finite projective measurement on $\\mathbb C^D$: its members are self-adjoint projections, they are mutually orthogonal, and their sum is $I_D$. Zero projections are allowed. Take the label sets $\\mathcal I_q$ disjoint, put $\\mathcal I=\\bigsqcup_{q\\in\\mathcal Q}\\mathcal I_q$, and set $n=|\\mathcal I|$. Suppose that*\n\n1.  *$P_iP_j=P_jP_i$ whenever $i\\in\\mathcal I_q$, $j\\in\\mathcal I_r$, and $c_{qr}\\ne0$;*\n\n2.  *there is a real number $\\kappa<Q/K$ such that every $J\\subseteq\\mathcal I$ satisfying $\\operatorname{tr}(P_iP_j)>0$ for all distinct $i,j\\in J$ has $|J|\\leq\\kappa$.*\n\n\n*After ordering $\\mathcal I$, define the complex-linear map $\\Psi:M_{n}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{D}(\\mathbb C)$ on matrix units by $$\\label{eq:projection-map}\n H_{ij}=c_{qr}P_iP_j,\\qquad\n \\Psi(E_{ij})=H_{ij}\n \\quad(i\\in\\mathcal I_q,\\ j\\in\\mathcal I_r).$$ Then $\\Psi$ and its Hilbert–Schmidt adjoint $\\Psi^\\dagger:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{n}(\\mathbb C)$ are PPT. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:projection-composite}\n C:=J(\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\Psi)\n \\quad\\text{is nonseparable over }\\mathbb C^n\\otimes\\mathbb C^n,\n \\qquad \\operatorname{tr}C=Q^2D.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Q,K,D$ be positive integers, let $\\mathcal Q$ be a set of $Q$ elements, and let $u_q\\in\\mathbb R^K$, $q\\in\\mathcal Q$, be unit vectors. Put $c_{qr}=u_q^{\\mathsf T}u_r$.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-projection-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "For each $q$, let $\\{P_i:i\\in\\mathcal I_q\\}$ be a finite projective measurement on $\\mathbb C^D$: its members are self-adjoint projections, they are mutually orthogonal, and their sum is $I_D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-projection-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "there is a real number $\\kappa<Q/K$ such that every $J\\subseteq\\mathcal I$ satisfying $\\operatorname{tr}(P_iP_j)>0$ for all distinct $i,j\\in J$ has $|J|\\leq\\kappa$.",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-projection-criterion"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-real-channel",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:projection-criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:projection-criterion\">26</a>, suppose that every $P_i$ is real symmetric in the fixed basis of $\\mathbb C^D$, and put $\\Lambda=\\Psi^\\dagger:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{n}(\\mathbb C)$. Then, on all complex inputs, $$\\label{eq:projection-real-invariances}\n \\Lambda\\circ T_D=\\Lambda=T_n\\circ\\Lambda,\n \\qquad \\operatorname{tr}\\Lambda(A)=Q\\,\\operatorname{tr}A.$$ In particular, $\\Lambda/Q$ is a trace-preserving PPT channel. For $\\Phi_D=\\Omega_D/\\sqrt D$, the normalized maximally entangled vector on $\\mathbb C^D\\otimes\\mathbb C^D$, one has $$\\label{eq:projection-real-state}\n \\frac{C}{Q^2D}\n =\n \\left(\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\otimes\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\right)\n       (\\Phi_D\\Phi_D^*).$$ This is an entangled density operator on $\\mathbb C^n\\otimes\\mathbb C^n$ in $\\mathscr C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-real-channel",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:projection-criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:projection-criterion\">26</a>, suppose that every $P_i$ is real symmetric in the fixed basis of $\\mathbb C^D$, and put $\\Lambda=\\Psi^\\dagger:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{n}(\\mathbb C)$. For $\\Phi_D=\\Omega_D/\\sqrt D$, the normalized maximally entangled vector on $\\mathbb C^D\\otimes\\mathbb C^D$, one has $$\\label{eq:projection-real-state}\n \\frac{C}{Q^2D}\n =\n \\left(\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\otimes\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\right)\n       (\\Phi_D\\Phi_D^*).$$ This is an entangled density operator on $\\mathbb C^n\\otimes\\mathbb C^n$ in $\\mathscr C$.",

      "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:projection-criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:projection-criterion\">26</a>, suppose that every $P_i$ is real symmetric in the fixed basis of $\\mathbb C^D$, and put $\\Lambda=\\Psi^\\dagger:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{n}(\\mathbb C)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-square-embedding",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:projection-criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:projection-criterion\">26</a>, suppose in addition that $n\\geq D$, and let $U:\\mathbb C^D\\to\\mathbb C^n$ be the real coordinate isometry onto the first $D$ coordinates. Define $$\\label{eq:projection-square-pair}\n \\Xi_1=\\operatorname{Ad}_U\\circ\\Psi,\\qquad\n \\Xi_2=\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\operatorname{Ad}_{U^*}\n       =\\Xi_1^\\dagger .$$ Then $\\Xi_1,\\Xi_2:M_{n}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{n}(\\mathbb C)$ are PPT and $$\\Xi_2\\circ\\Xi_1=\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\Psi.$$ Their composition is not entanglement breaking.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-square-embedding",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:projection-criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:projection-criterion\">26</a>, suppose in addition that $n\\geq D$, and let $U:\\mathbb C^D\\to\\mathbb C^n$ be the real coordinate isometry onto the first $D$ coordinates.       "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:projection-criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:projection-criterion\">26</a>, suppose in addition that $n\\geq D$, and let $U:\\mathbb C^D\\to\\mathbb C^n$ be the real coordinate isometry onto the first $D$ coordinates.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-old-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Set $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-exact-data}\n \\begin{gathered}\n p=257,\\qquad k=1028,\\qquad\n b=\\binom{1027}{513},\\qquad\n a_0=\\sum_{j=0}^{256}\\binom{1028}{j},\\\\\n Q=b^3,\\qquad K=1086373952,\\qquad\n \\kappa=3a_0b^2<\\frac QK,\\\\\n L=\\binom Q8,\\qquad D=4^L,\\qquad m=QD.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Use the vectors in <a href=\"#eq:combinatorial-questions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:combinatorial-questions\">[eq:combinatorial-questions]</a>, the $D$ labels per question and projections in <a href=\"#eq:combinatorial-global-projections\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:combinatorial-global-projections\">[eq:combinatorial-global-projections]</a>, and the map $\\Psi:M_{m}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{D}(\\mathbb C)$ in <a href=\"#eq:projection-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projection-map\">[eq:projection-map]</a>. Put $\\Lambda=\\Psi^\\dagger$ and $\\Phi_D=\\Omega_D/\\sqrt D$.*\n\n\n1.  *The map $\\Lambda/Q:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$ is a trace-preserving PPT channel, and $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-state}\n            \\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}\n            =\\left(\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\otimes\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\right)\n              (\\Phi_D\\Phi_D^*)\n            =\\frac{J(\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\Psi)}{Q^2D}$$ is an entangled density operator on $\\mathbb C^m\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$ belonging to $\\mathscr C$. It has $K_D(\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}})=0$ and satisfies the uniform unhalved $1/5$ gap and all operational consequences of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:zero-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:zero-key\">17</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:operational-extensions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:operational-extensions\">18</a>, with their stated protocol, purification, and completion hypotheses.*\n\n\n2.  *For the real coordinate isometry $U:\\mathbb C^D\\to\\mathbb C^m$ onto the first $D$ coordinates, the maps $$\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}}=\\operatorname{Ad}_U\\circ\\Psi,\\qquad\n            \\Phi_2^{\\mathrm{comb}}=\\Lambda\\circ\\operatorname{Ad}_{U^*}\n                     =(\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}})^\\dagger$$ are PPT maps on $M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$, and $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-pair}\n            J(\\Phi_2^{\\mathrm{comb}}\\circ\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}})\n              =Q^2D\\,\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}$$ is nonseparable over $\\mathbb C^m\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$. In particular their composition is not entanglement breaking.*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}$ be the channel obtained by applying Proposition <a href=\"#prop:channel-completion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:channel-completion\">25</a> to this square pair. It is a trace-preserving PPT channel on $M_{2m+1}(\\mathbb C)$. With the positive constants $c_1,c_2$ and the first coordinate inclusion $W_0$ from that proposition, the Choi matrix of its square has the corner $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-channel-corner}\n            (W_0^*\\otimes W_0^*)J(\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}^2)\n            (W_0\\otimes W_0)\n            =c_1c_2Q^2D\\,\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}.$$ Consequently $\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}^2\n             =\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}\\circ\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}$ is not entanglement breaking.",
        "source_id": "PM-272.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-old-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Set $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-exact-data}\n \\begin{gathered}\n p=257,\\qquad k=1028,\\qquad\n b=\\binom{1027}{513},\\qquad\n a_0=\\sum_{j=0}^{256}\\binom{1028}{j},\\\\\n Q=b^3,\\qquad K=1086373952,\\qquad\n \\kappa=3a_0b^2<\\frac QK,\\\\\n L=\\binom Q8,\\qquad D=4^L,\\qquad m=QD.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Use the vectors in <a href=\"#eq:combinatorial-questions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:combinatorial-questions\">[eq:combinatorial-questions]</a>, the $D$ labels per question and projections in <a href=\"#eq:combinatorial-global-projections\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:combinatorial-global-projections\">[eq:combinatorial-global-projections]</a>, and the map $\\Psi:M_{m}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{D}(\\mathbb C)$ in <a href=\"#eq:projection-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projection-map\">[eq:projection-map]</a>. Put $\\Lambda=\\Psi^\\dagger$ and $\\Phi_D=\\Omega_D/\\sqrt D$.*\n\n\n1.  *The map $\\Lambda/Q:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$ is a trace-preserving PPT channel, and $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-state}\n            \\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}\n            =\\left(\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\otimes\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\right)\n              (\\Phi_D\\Phi_D^*)\n            =\\frac{J(\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\Psi)}{Q^2D}$$ is an entangled density operator on $\\mathbb C^m\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$ belonging to $\\mathscr C$. It has $K_D(\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}})=0$ and satisfies the uniform unhalved $1/5$ gap and all operational consequences of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:zero-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:zero-key\">17</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:operational-extensions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:operational-extensions\">18</a>, with their stated protocol, purification, and completion hypotheses.*\n\n\n2.  *For the real coordinate isometry $U:\\mathbb C^D\\to\\mathbb C^m$ onto the first $D$ coordinates, the maps $$\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}}=\\operatorname{Ad}_U\\circ\\Psi,\\qquad\n            \\Phi_2^{\\mathrm{comb}}=\\Lambda\\circ\\operatorname{Ad}_{U^*}\n                     =(\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}})^\\dagger$$ are PPT maps on $M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$, and $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-pair}\n            J(\\Phi_2^{\\mathrm{comb}}\\circ\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}})\n              =Q^2D\\,\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}$$ is nonseparable over $\\mathbb C^m\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$. In particular their composition is not entanglement breaking.*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}$ be the channel obtained by applying Proposition <a href=\"#prop:channel-completion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:channel-completion\">25</a> to this square pair. It is a trace-preserving PPT channel on $M_{2m+1}(\\mathbb C)$. With the positive constants $c_1,c_2$ and the first coordinate inclusion $W_0$ from that proposition, the Choi matrix of its square has the corner $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-channel-corner}\n            (W_0^*\\otimes W_0^*)J(\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}^2)\n            (W_0\\otimes W_0)\n            =c_1c_2Q^2D\\,\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}.$$ Consequently $\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}^2\n             =\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}\\circ\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}$ is not entanglement breaking.",
          "source_id": "PM-272.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Set $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-exact-data}\n \\begin{gathered}\n p=257,\\qquad k=1028,\\qquad\n b=\\binom{1027}{513},\\qquad\n a_0=\\sum_{j=0}^{256}\\binom{1028}{j},\\\\\n Q=b^3,\\qquad K=1086373952,\\qquad\n \\kappa=3a_0b^2<\\frac QK,\\\\\n L=\\binom Q8,\\qquad D=4^L,\\qquad m=QD.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Use the vectors in <a href=\"#eq:combinatorial-questions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:combinatorial-questions\">[eq:combinatorial-questions]</a>, the $D$ labels per question and projections in <a href=\"#eq:combinatorial-global-projections\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:combinatorial-global-projections\">[eq:combinatorial-global-projections]</a>, and the map $\\Psi:M_{m}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{D}(\\mathbb C)$ in <a href=\"#eq:projection-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projection-map\">[eq:projection-map]</a>. Put $\\Lambda=\\Psi^\\dagger$ and $\\Phi_D=\\Omega_D/\\sqrt D$.*\n\n\n1.  *The map $\\Lambda/Q:M_{D}(\\mathbb C)\\to M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$ is a trace-preserving PPT channel, and $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-state}\n            \\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}\n            =\\left(\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\otimes\\frac{\\Lambda}{Q}\\right)\n              (\\Phi_D\\Phi_D^*)\n            =\\frac{J(\\Psi^\\dagger\\circ\\Psi)}{Q^2D}$$ is an entangled density operator on $\\mathbb C^m\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$ belonging to $\\mathscr C$. It has $K_D(\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}})=0$ and satisfies the uniform unhalved $1/5$ gap and all operational consequences of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:zero-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:zero-key\">17</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:operational-extensions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:operational-extensions\">18</a>, with their stated protocol, purification, and completion hypotheses.*\n\n\n2.  *For the real coordinate isometry $U:\\mathbb C^D\\to\\mathbb C^m$ onto the first $D$ coordinates, the maps $$\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}}=\\operatorname{Ad}_U\\circ\\Psi,\\qquad\n            \\Phi_2^{\\mathrm{comb}}=\\Lambda\\circ\\operatorname{Ad}_{U^*}\n                     =(\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}})^\\dagger$$ are PPT maps on $M_{m}(\\mathbb C)$, and $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-pair}\n            J(\\Phi_2^{\\mathrm{comb}}\\circ\\Phi_1^{\\mathrm{comb}})\n              =Q^2D\\,\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}$$ is nonseparable over $\\mathbb C^m\\otimes\\mathbb C^m$. In particular their composition is not entanglement breaking.*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}$ be the channel obtained by applying Proposition <a href=\"#prop:channel-completion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:channel-completion\">25</a> to this square pair. It is a trace-preserving PPT channel on $M_{2m+1}(\\mathbb C)$. With the positive constants $c_1,c_2$ and the first coordinate inclusion $W_0$ from that proposition, the Choi matrix of its square has the corner $$\\label{eq:combinatorial-channel-corner}\n            (W_0^*\\otimes W_0^*)J(\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}^2)\n            (W_0\\otimes W_0)\n            =c_1c_2Q^2D\\,\\rho_{\\mathrm{comb}}.$$ Consequently $\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}^2\n             =\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}\\circ\\Theta_{\\mathrm{comb}}$ is not entanglement breaking.",
      "explanation": "The finite combinatorial formulas produce an entangled represented state from identical parallel rectangular channel actions, a square PPT pair with nonseparable composite Choi matrix, and a trace-preserving PPT channel with non-entanglement-breaking square. The state inherits the operational zero-key obstruction.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Represented class** ([source](#stmt-def-represented-class)): A density operator $\omega$ on $\mathbb C^a\otimes\mathbb C^b$ belongs to the represented class $\mathscr C$ if there are positive integers $r,s$, a vector $\chi\in\mathbb C^r\otimes\mathbb C^s$, and linear maps $\mathcal L:M_{r}(\mathbb C)\to M_{a}(\mathbb C)$ and $\mathcal M:M_{s}(\mathbb C)\to M_{b}(\mathbb C)$ such that $$\label{eq:represented-class} \omega=(\mathcal L\otimes\mathcal M)(\chi\chi^*), \qquad \mathcal L,\ \mathcal L\circ T_r,\ \mathcal M,\ \mathcal M\circ T_s \text{ are CP}.$$ Only $\omega$ is required to have trace one. The vector $\chi$ may be unnormalized, and the maps need not preserve trace.
- **Admitted local protocol** ([source](#stmt-protocol-completion)): Let $A$ and $B$ be finite-dimensional complex input spaces. An admitted protocol consists of local quantum instruments, local memories and randomness, and authenticated two-way public classical communication. Each party may process its whole input space jointly. Its data and their joint laws satisfy the following requirements. 1. *Initial resources.* Before interaction, there is a recorded standard Borel public variable $C_0$, possibly constant, whose law is fixed once the protocol is chosen. It is independent of the supplied input together with any purifying or untouched reference. Conditional on $C_0$, the joint state or law of that input and reference and the two initial private auxiliary resources is the product of the input-reference state and one resource for each laboratory. These local resources include private ancillas and randomness and may depend on $C_0$. The public law and these two preparation kernels are the same whenever this protocol is run on another input. All shared initial randomness is included in $C_0$. An initialization acting on a party’s input is treated as a first local operation. Apart from this recorded public variable and the supplied input, there is no shared resource and no preshared secret key. 2. *Local instruments.* An instrument assigns completely positive operations to measurable outcome sets, countably additively on input states. It has measurable outcome laws and measurable conditional local updates, and its total operation is trace preserving. Its classical outcome space is standard Borel. Local instruments may depend measurably on the local private history and the preceding public record. 3. *Local aggregation and chronological history.* A local subroutine completed before a later step may be aggregated into one instrument when that aggregate is supplied as a local instrument reproducing its retained output and reference law on arbitrary inputs with untouched finite-dimensional references. It includes every private record later used and its continuing memory. Its total is normalized, including a nonreturn or failure tag when needed. All public communication remains in the public schedule. After any such aggregation and before a separate terminal readout on a complete infinite history, the genuine classical history, including private instrument outcomes and public records, forms a finite sequence or a sequence indexed by $\mathbb N$, in fixed standard Borel coordinate spaces. Instrument-choice tags and fixed padding for stopped histories are included in these spaces. A local choice uses only that laboratory’s preceding private history and the preceding public record; any choice affecting the other laboratory is communicated through a public step. 4. *Public steps and the complete record.* The public discussion is correspondingly finite or indexed by $\mathbb N$. Each message is produced by its sender’s local process from that sender’s private history and the preceding public record. For a later public-source draw after the public record $T_m=t$, the recorded outcome is drawn from a measurable probability kernel $\kappa_m(t,dc)$ depending only on that record. On every unnormalized joint instrument law, this public update multiplies by the scalar kernel and acts as the identity on the quantum and reference registers. The kernel at a given record is the same for every initial input. A party-generated announced choice, deterministic or randomized, is that party’s message. Public choices of speaker and public stopping decisions use these same two rules; a choice measurable from the preceding public record is a Dirac public kernel. Simultaneous messages may be ordered while retaining their dependence on the earlier record. Every publicly observed control or other datum is generated and recorded by one of these steps. The standard Borel record $T$ is the encoded sequence of $C_0$ and all these public messages, draws, speaker choices, and stopping decisions. Private outcomes and quantum memories remain in their own laboratories. 5. *Reference-compatible prefix laws.* The specified steps are one family of instruments on arbitrary positive trace-class inputs, with their amplifications by any untouched finite-dimensional reference. Their unnormalized branch laws give countably additive positive matrix-valued measures on that reference for finite prefixes. Integrating the next record recovers the preceding reference measure after the current laboratory memories are traced out. 6. *Terminal outputs and local quantum readouts.* A terminal classical output may be a measurable function of that laboratory’s instrument history and the received complete transcript $T$. A decision to invoke a terminal quantum readout must be measurable in the same local records. For a terminal quantum readout, the protocol supplies both its incoming local memory and the instrument applied to it. The memory is supplied by one fixed measurable local CP process on those histories, defined on arbitrary inputs with any untouched finite-dimensional reference. The readout is a specified measurable local instrument on that memory, selected using only that laboratory’s private history and $T$. The two laboratories refine the reference measure on the complete history by these local operations, acting as the identity on the reference. Together with the preceding schedule, these refinements give one countably additive, unnormalized instrument law on the complete history and the terminal outcomes, for all initial inputs and finite untouched references. Include a tag for a missing or noninvoked readout among each laboratory’s outcomes. Summing either laboratory’s terminal outcomes removes that local refinement. Summing both and tracing the output memories recovers the reference measure on the complete history with its stated finite-prefix marginals. After any terminal outcomes have been included, each prescribed output status is a measurable local value in the finite output alphabet together with a symbol $\perp$ for nonproduction. An output already produced is retained. The incoming-memory and compatibility requirements concern only memory used by a terminal readout; unused memory need not converge. An unspecified evolution through infinitely many quantum operations does not by itself define a terminal readout. 7. *Completion on a specified input.* A *completed bit-output protocol on a specified input* has a bit in each laboratory almost surely on that input, with normalized joint output. Almost-sure production is required only on this actual input. More generally a completed key-output protocol produces an output in a specified finite classical alphabet at each party almost surely on the actual input, again with normalized joint output. Trace-norm limits of completed actual outputs, with full recoverable public information, will be treated separately in Section <a href="#sec:secret-key" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:secret-key">5</a>.
- **Distillable secret key** ([source](#stmt-def-distillable-key)): Let $\omega$ be a bipartite state, with Eve initially holding a purification. A key-distillation sequence uses an admitted protocol of Definition <a href="#def:protocol" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:protocol">[def:protocol]</a> on each $\omega^{\otimes n}$, allowing each party to process all its $n$ copies jointly. The normalized classical output at each party is a string of a fixed length $\ell_n\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$ for that $n$. Eve’s output register $E'$ retains the initial purification and the complete public transcript. For any normalized state or classical-quantum law $\sigma_{E'}$, define the ideal length-$\ell$ key by $$\gamma^{(\ell)}_\sigma =2^{-\ell}\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{\ell}} \|z,z\rangle\langle z,z\|\otimes\sigma_{E'}.$$ The sequence is secure if there are normalized comparison laws $\sigma_{E'}^{(n)}$ such that $$\label{eq:key-definition} \left\lVert\tau^{(n)}_{K_AK_BE'}- \gamma^{(\ell_n)}_{\sigma^{(n)}}\right\rVert_1\longrightarrow0.$$ Its asymptotic lower rate is $\liminf_{n\to\infty}\ell_n/n$. The distillable secret key $K_D(\omega)$ is the supremum of these rates over secure sequences, including the zero rate obtained by producing the empty key. A positive rate therefore requires $\ell_n\ge1$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
- **Input-first unnormalized Choi matrix** ([source](#eq:choi-definition)): For $F:M_{a}(\mathbb C)\to M_{b}(\mathbb C)$ our Choi convention is $$\label{eq:choi-definition} J(F)=\sum_{i,j=1}^a E_{ij}\otimes F(E_{ij}) =(\mathrm{id}_a\otimes F)(\Omega_a\Omega_a^*), \qquad \Omega_a=\sum_{i=1}^a e_i\otimes e_i.$$
- **Unnormalized root fidelity** ([source](#sec:secret-key)): $$ Its asymptotic lower rate is $\liminf_{n\to\infty}\ell_n/n$. The distillable secret key $K_D(\omega)$ is the supremum of these rates over secure sequences, including the zero rate obtained by producing the empty key. A positive rate therefore requires $\ell_n\ge1$ for all sufficiently large $n$. </div> For positive matrices define the unnormalized root fidelity by $$f(H,J)=\left\lVert\sqrt H\sqrt J\right\rVert_1.$$
- **Canonical purification** ([source](#eq:canonical-purification)): Let $R$ be the input state on finite-dimensional spaces $A\otimes B$. In fixed product bases, Eve’s canonical purification is $$\label{eq:canonical-purification} \|\Psi_R\rangle=\sum_h\sqrt R\,\|h\rangle_{AB}\|h\rangle_E, \qquad E\cong A\otimes B.$$
- **Symmetric quadratic vector** ([source](#sec:geometry)): Put $$\widehat x=U^*(x\otimes x) =\sum_i x_i^2e_{ii}+\sqrt2\sum_{i<j}x_ix_je_{ij} \qquad(x\in\mathbb C^4). \label{eq:veronese}$$
- **Complementary-pair exterior operator** ([source](#sec:geometry)): For $i<j$, let $k<l$ be the complementary indices, and let $\epsilon_{ijkl}$ be the sign of the ordering $(i,j,k,l)$ of $(0,1,2,3)$. Define the real signed permutation $K$ on $\mathbb C^6$ by $$Ke_{ij}=\epsilon_{ijkl}e_{kl}. \label{eq:complement}$$
- **Integer-pencil map** ([source](#eq:L)): For $x=(x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)\in\mathbb C^4$, put $M(x)=\sum_{i=0}^3x_iM_i$ and define the complex-linear map $L$ on matrix units by $$L(E_{ij})=M_i^{\mathsf T}M_j\qquad(0\leq i,j<4). \label{eq:L}$$
- **Affine rank-loss quotient** ([source](#pm-h-24)): Put $p_\rho(t)=\Delta_\rho(1,t_1,t_2,t_3)$ and define $$I=(p_\rho)_\rho\subset\mathbb Q[t_1,t_2,t_3],\qquad \mathcal A=\mathbb Q[t_1,t_2,t_3]/I.$$
- **Projection-overlap map** ([source](#eq:projection-map)): *After ordering $\mathcal I$, define the complex-linear map $\Psi:M_{n}(\mathbb C)\to M_{D}(\mathbb C)$ on matrix units by $$\label{eq:projection-map} H_{ij}=c_{qr}P_iP_j,\qquad \Psi(E_{ij})=H_{ij} \quad(i\in\mathcal I_q,\ j\in\mathcal I_r).$$
- **Orthogonal eight-set** ([source](#eq:combinatorial-question-gram)): An *orthogonal eight-set* will mean an eight-element subset of $\mathcal Q$ whose associated vectors are pairwise orthogonal.

The two laboratories refine the reference measure on the complete history by these local operations, acting as the identity on the reference.\n\n    Together with the preceding schedule, these refinements give one countably additive, unnormalized instrument law on the complete history and the terminal outcomes, for all initial inputs and finite untouched references. Include a tag for a missing or noninvoked readout among each laboratory’s outcomes. Summing either laboratory’s terminal outcomes removes that local refinement. Summing both and tracing the output memories recovers the reference measure on the complete history with its stated finite-prefix marginals.\n\n    After any terminal outcomes have been included, each prescribed output status is a measurable local value in the finite output alphabet together with a symbol $\\perp$ for nonproduction. An output already produced is retained. The incoming-memory and compatibility requirements concern only memory used by a terminal readout; unused memory need not converge. An unspecified evolution through infinitely many quantum operations does not by itself define a terminal readout.\n\n7.  *Completion on a specified input.* A *completed bit-output protocol on a specified input* has a bit in each laboratory almost surely on that input, with normalized joint output. Almost-sure production is required only on this actual input. More generally a completed key-output protocol produces an output in a specified finite classical alphabet at each party almost surely on the actual input, again with normalized joint output. Trace-norm limits of completed actual outputs, with full recoverable public information, will be treated separately in Section <a href=\"#sec:secret-key\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:secret-key\">5</a>.",

The normalized classical output at each party is a string of a fixed length $\\ell_n\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}$ for that $n$. Eve’s output register $E'$ retains the initial purification and the complete public transcript. For any normalized state or classical-quantum law $\\sigma_{E'}$, define the ideal length-$\\ell$ key by $$\\gamma^{(\\ell)}_\\sigma\n =2^{-\\ell}\\sum_{z\\in\\{0,1\\}^{\\ell}}\n     |z,z\\rangle\\langle z,z|\\otimes\\sigma_{E'}.$$ The sequence is secure if there are normalized comparison laws $\\sigma_{E'}^{(n)}$ such that $$\\label{eq:key-definition}\n \\left\\lVert\\tau^{(n)}_{K_AK_BE'}-\n        \\gamma^{(\\ell_n)}_{\\sigma^{(n)}}\\right\\rVert_1\\longrightarrow0.$$ Its asymptotic lower rate is $\\liminf_{n\\to\\infty}\\ell_n/n$. The distillable secret key $K_D(\\omega)$ is the supremum of these rates over secure sequences, including the zero rate obtained by producing the empty key.
